“He must understand this is the absolute maximum the government can offer,” Johanson said. “Now his fate rests with you, Dada. You have to convince him.”

I shrugged my shoulders and sighed, saying, “I’ll do my best.”

After leaving the UN office, we took a taxi together with Suresh’s father. On the way, he suddenly exploded. “Whose side are you on?” he yelled. “I think you prefer my son’s death to the reasonable option given by Johanson! You are the cause of all this horror! You don’t really care about Stan!” He went on screaming similar things, giving me no chance to reply, until at last he broke down weeping into his hands.

Karan and I grimaced in pain. He gently rubbed Mr Eklof’s back. We drove in silence, except for the sound of the old man’s sobs.

The taxi arrived at his house. Just before he got out, I said, “Please believe me, Mr Eklof. I love Stan deeply.”

Mr Eklof’s face showed exhaustion. He gave a weak smile, turned and walked toward his house.

I felt awful. I felt so bad for Mr Eklof that I had physical pain in my heart.

Later Suresh called again. “You people are working so hard. And I’m here in hiding, carefree and enjoying my

meditation. Dadaji. I hope these days are not too tiring for you all. Rest assured that I’m happy, happier than ever.”

“But you know, Suresh, if you don’t accept the government’s offer, you may be doomed. They insist this is the limit.”

“I understand, Dadaji. I expected it might be like this from the moment I first announced my resolution. If I die, it will provide just the right sort of shock to humanity.”

“Suresh, you can do infinitely more for humanity if you go on living and serving.”

“I don’t know, Dadaji, and I don’t care. I feel deeply that I’m doing exactly what Baba wants. I’m receiving His immeasurable grace every moment. The entire cosmos seems to be His smile, especially when I do meditation. So I have no doubt.”

“If you think like that, then what are we to do?”

“What you are already doing is perfect. Please continue your efforts, unattached to their results. In the end it will happen according to His whim.”

“Suresh....”

“I know it’s harder for you all than for me, but I shall accept no compromise.”

The following day. “Damn it,” said Johanson, “he’s asking for the impossible.”

“I know,” I said. “He’s fully prepared to die.”

“Can’t you provide any further lead as to where Eklof might be? Our investigators say they’ve already checked every imaginable link, and nothing remains except to search every house in the country.”

“No. No further clue. And I already told you that finding him is immaterial. Wherever you might keep him, however you try to restrain him, you’ll never dissuade him from his resolution. I tell you, there’s no other way. The only real solution is for Sweden to present Mr Sarkar’s case to the United Nations.”

He didn’t reply. We sat in silence for one or two minutes.

Then he said, “Sir, that’s enough for this morning. If possible, please be in your office this afternoon, as I may need to call you.”

Johanson did call in the afternoon, and completely astonished me.

After hanging up, I waited impatiently for Suresh to phone.

Thirty minutes later, the phone rang. It was Suresh.

“The Swedish government’s decided to put Baba’s case to the UN!” I blurted out. “Can you believe it, Suresh? Congratulations!”

*“Tato Baba, tato dharma. Tato dharma, tato jaya,”* he said quietly and calmly. “Where there is Baba, there is righteousness. And where there is righteousness, there is victory.”

“I want to see you as soon as possible, Suresh. We should meet Mr Johanson also. Which is closer for you—our office or the UN office?”

“I can meet you in the lobby of the UN office,” he said. “It’s only a few minutes walk from where I am now.”

“You crafty fellow! Exactly where have you been staying?”

“In the apartment of an old friend. By ‘old’ I mean he’s more than 80 years old. And he never reads the newspaper or listens to the radio news. I told him my house is being painted. Ha, ha!”

Before I left, Karan called Suresh’s father. When he heard the news, he burst out, “Oh, thank God! Thank God!” He cried a bit, and then said, “Please accept my apology for what I said in the taxi yesterday. Without your efforts, I think my son would have died.”

After waiting for ten minutes or so in the UN office lobby, I began to feel a bit nervous. Suresh should have arrived before me.

Another man had also been standing there for a long time.

Might he have seen Suresh? I approached him. He smiled.

“For God’s sake, it’s you, Suresh!”

He ripped off his false mustache, took off his bogus spectacles, and burst out laughing. We embraced each other, weeping like babies.

And that was that.

### His invisible hand

Talk about no experience. It was one thing to start a food cooperative; but it is an entirely different ball game to open a public printing press without the slightest knowledge. There lay the faith of the devotees in Oslo. Bhagirath and Arjuna were confident that their enterprise would be successful. Was it not wonderful that all of our projects offered on-the-job training? And most of them did so without any trainer! Unless you counted the Supreme Trainer.

In Copenhagen, a soup kitchen was opened for poor people.

In Stockholm, service projects were multiplying like rabbits. Akashi opened a health-food cooperative which had an atmosphere that could be created only by such a refined lady.

I had not physically seen Baba yet. Neither had most of the Margiis. Still, I knew Him intimately and so did most of the Margiis. Part of that came from kiirtan and meditation, but honestly, it was mostly through service that we really got to enjoy His company. I saw Baba in my work, in the eyes of the people I met, in the constant helpful ‘coincidences’ that occurred every day, in the struggles that tested me and helped me grow. After all, what else did I have? Though He insisted on depriving me of His physical presence, I still had the right to demand that He show Himself to me in my daily life. Of course, I would forego all that in a moment in exchange for letting me just once have His personal contact.

### An embassy becomes a guerrilla theater

Due to the imposition of martial law in India, Baba’s case, which was already a travesty of justice, had turned into an

unambiguous farce. Not a single witness from Ananda Marga’s side was permitted to testify. By kangaroo-court, Baba had now been declared guilty of all charges. At first, the judge declared the sentence to be the death penalty. Later, fearing Baba might be seen in the same light as Socrates or Christ, he changed his mind, and converted the sentence to life imprisonment. In a way this alteration allowed us to feel a certain sort of comfort.[^1]

My spiritual father was unquestionably suffering, while fasting for years in a cell devoid of all conveniences. At the same time, thousands of my Indian brothers and sisters were also undergoing daily agony in scores of other prisons as they refused to give up their commitment to Ananda Marga’s cause.[^2]

And here I was in Copenhagen. In the face of this horror, however, I remained calm, even inspired. Though it might seem heartless, I was convinced that Baba was causing the whole drama to take place, according to His plan. Sometimes great suffering is necessary though it is difficult to accept and even more difficult to live through. Despite the horror, despite the difficulties, I had faith that everything would turn out for the best.

Rather than give in to despair or anger, it was far better to keep a cool mind, and actively protest the conditions in order to help the public become more conscious. For Margiis, the norm during these days was public demonstrations, letter-writing campaigns, and meetings with influential figures throughout the world.

Even though respected and sometimes famous individuals and organizations had investigated our cases, and had objected in detail to the flagrant injustices taking place, the Indian

[^1]: Not long after, both hands of this judge became permanently paralyzed.
[^2]: Two years later, a book was published entitled, *Tales of Torture*, which documented scores of cases of Dadas, Didis and Margiis who were severely tormented physically and mentally by the jail officers. The twenty-two months of emergency in India were notorious for incalculable human rights violations.

authorities remained unaffected. The Canadian representative of the International Commission of Jurists made a lengthy report, spelling out how appalled he was by the bias against Ananda Marga. And a high-ranking Queen’s Counsel from England pointed out over 200 loopholes in the prosecution’s position in Baba’s case. We had unquestionable evidence that the Foreign Office in Delhi sent several anti-Ananda Marga information packets to Indian ambassadors and embassy staff of the world. The embassies had been instructed to distribute these packets to government officials in their respective countries so that Ananda Marga’s development might be impeded.

Accordingly, we continued to protest in every way that we could.

Seven of us gathered one morning at the Indian embassy. We had informed a reporter of the biggest Danish daily newspaper of our intentions. The reporter also came, but he refused to go inside with us. He said that he would get the news after we came out.

It was chilly weather, so we were all dressed in full-length coats. The first Margii entered alone, and began reading an Indian newspaper in the reception room. After a few minutes, a second Margii went inside and studied the visa application forms. Gradually, one by one, the rest of us entered and engaged ourselves in inconspicuous behavior. The usual staff was there, together with a handful of other people.

Then one of us gave the signal. In a flash, we all jumped up, pulled signs from under our coats and launched into a continuous series of loud chants—“Out with martial law!”, “Arrest Indira Gandhi!”, “Free Baba now!”, “Release political prisoners!”, “Ananda Marga demands justice!” The staff was utterly shocked.

The lady employees began screaming at the top of their lungs. Some dived under the tables. Clearly they thought that they were under some sort of attack—perhaps that our

continuous chants were the prelude to a spray of machine gun bullets or something. They screamed non-stop. I was really sorry for them, but I knew that they would recover within a few minutes. Well, I know it sounds terrible and cruel, but I must be honest. While one side of my brain was in pain, feeling compassion for these hapless souls, the other side, please excuse me for saying so, felt like laughing. I wonder if God at times felt something similar when He viewed our melodramas.

In the midst of this, the Indian ambassador himself appeared at the top of the stairs. I feared for his eyes which looked like they might dislodge themselves from their sockets. Before that could happen, however, he and two or three of his aids galloped down the stairs and, with a roar, started raining their fists on our innocent bodies. One of the Margiis deftly pulled out a camera, and snapped the scene again and again. When their attack failed to stop our parade, the ambassador flew into a rage at our camera-man, and tried his level best to expose the film by seizing the camera and throwing it to the floor. Though we were quite civil, I should even say polite (considering his discourtesy) in fulfilling our task, he pushed Kunti toward the door, utilizing every drop of the adrenaline which was pumping through his bloodstream. Perhaps worrying that some harm might be done to the door, a male staff member opened it, and the ambassador succeeded in tossing Kunti out. The reporter was standing there. Catching just the right moment, he snapped a photo.

I was sure that the ambassador must have rejoiced to see the photo on the front page of the newspaper in the evening. His face wore a frozen vicious scowl while he was thrusting Kunti onto the sidewalk. Of course, she had on her best expression of childlike astonishment at his uncouth behavior. The article was perfect, nicely detailing the injustice perpetrated by the Indian government on Baba and Ananda Marga.

### Fate twisting

All the Dadas and Didis of Berlin Sector were gathered in

Verona, Italy, for several days of meetings.

A visitor was also there: my father. It was the first time that I had seen him since I left home over four years ago.

During the few days we had together, we took every chance we could to get away from the others and talk.

I initiated him into meditation. We were sitting on a blanket, under a bright sky, getting ready to begin, when he started laughing.

"What makes you laugh in this serious moment?"

He swallowed his mirth and said, "I'm sorry, sonny boy. But the irony is too much. In all truth, I admit I came here to convince you to give up this life, and return to America. But here I am, perched like a holy Hindu, about to acquire the esoteric knowledge from you. I thought I would convince you, but instead, you've convinced me."

### Scandinavian zeal

It was 1977. In the last few months, I was initiating up to 200 persons monthly. An immense number of new service projects also started, including three free kitchens, a touring art exhibit, a touring drama group, yoga classes in three prisons, two kindergartens, a herb farm, and the regular publication of two magazines.

# CHAPTER 8

## *Personal Contact*

### Indira and her emergency both finish

March 1977. Indira Gandhi’s “emergency” in India finally ended! She over-estimated her popularity, permitted elections to be held—and lost by the biggest landslide in Indian history, receiving only a few percent of the vote. Already reports were coming in that all our workers and Margiis were being released from jail because Ananda Marga was legal again. They tried to crush us, but our movement had only been strengthened through facing and overcoming persecution.

Now it only remained for Baba and a few specially accused workers to be released. With the new Janata Party government in power, I was hopeful that their cases would soon come up for appeal.

I remember something very interesting. Two and a half years prior to this, Baba had issued a warning from prison: “After six months, a crisis will occur in India. All Margii families should store sufficient cereals and basic necessities to weather a period of two years.”

At the time we all thought that He meant an earthquake or war was coming. Six months later, Indira Gandhi announced

the emergency, and most of the fathers in Margii households throughout India were thrown into jail. The Margii mothers and children were left to fend for themselves. Thanks to Baba’s warning, however, most of them had stored sufficient food for this period, which turned out to last for nearly two years.[^1]

Meanwhile, on our side of the world everything continued better than ever. In Reykjavik, Iceland, Ranjan and Vimala had opened a health food cooperative. This country hardly knew the expression “health food”, and our shop was the first of its kind. Nevertheless, even on its first day, the shop was already full of customers.

### The bones of the immoralists will shake

Patna, India. This was the third time that I had been to India since becoming an acharya. Hundreds, perhaps thousands of Margiis had visited Baba in Bankipur Prison.[^2] Only five Dadas from around the world, including me, were on a special prison black-list which did not permit us to enter His cell. Though I had applied many times for visiting privileges, the prison authorities always refused. Twice I had met with the prison superintendent, only to receive the same reply.

For some reason, we select few were considered dangerous. It was surely due to the misinformation that commonly filled secret police files. One of our Norwegian Margiis was able to

[^1]: Another story was later told to me by Brij Bihari and retold by Dada Pranavanandananda. It happened in 1971, when Brij was Baba’s attendant during the short time that Baba was in the hospital. While entering the bathroom, with the door still half open, Baba nonchalantly said, “In 1975 our organization will face a severe crisis. Even if you travel miles and miles, you will not meet anyone who will admit to being a Margii. It will be one of the most testing periods of the Marga.” Without further comment, He closed the door. Brij mentioned the episode to several other workers and Margiis then, but no one knew what to make of it.

[^2]: Though Ananda Marga was by now legal and again functioning in India, Baba’s case was still not resolved. The new Janata government ordered the courts to retry all cases decided during Gandhi’s rule of martial law. This process was, of course, taking time.

gain access to Interpol’s files through the help of a relative who worked in Norway’s undercover agent section. When he checked my name, he found numerous false statements, including one declaring that I was sending $5000 every month by bank transfer to a revolutionary fund in America. The fact is that I had never sent even one cent to America.

On this particular day, I decided to appeal directly to the Governor of the State of Bihar. When I arrived at the State Office, I explained that I was a Margii seeking an interview with the Governor.

“The Governor is now out,” the receptionist said, “but I’ll see if you can meet the Vice-Governor.”

She left and came back after a few minutes.

“I’m sorry, Sir. The Vice-Governor is too busy today.”

“How about tomorrow?”

“It won’t be possible,” she said.

“Alright, I can wait a few days. Just set the time.”

“I’m sorry, sir. Please don’t mind, but he just doesn’t want to meet you.”

I thanked her and walked out. After waiting outside for fifteen minutes, I walked in again. While the receptionist was diverted by another person, I walked past her unannounced.

Because I moved with seeming confidence through the halls of the building, no one questioned me. Having no idea where the Vice-Governor’s office was, I entered one corridor after another until I finally found the proper door. Several people were sitting on a bench, waiting. I joined them without a word.

A few minutes passed, and then the door opened. Someone came out. Before the door closed, I stood up and walked inside.

It was a big room, decorated in rich aristocratic fashion. The Vice-Governor was sitting alone at his desk.

He was clearly surprised to receive a visitor without prior notification.

“How can I help you, sir?” Perhaps because I was a foreigner his tone was respectful.

“I am sorry to disturb you,” I said softly. “I am a Margii seeking permission to visit my guru, Shrii P.R. Sarkar, who is presently in Bankipur Jail.”

He started shaking slightly.

“You should follow the normal channels with the prison authorities,” he said.

“I have already exhausted those. That’s why I came to you.”

“Only the Governor himself can deal with your case. He’s in Delhi, so you can contact him there, please. Thank you, and good bye.”

“In this situation, sir,” I said, “it is you that should call him. Besides, I have information indicating that you have full power in his absence to take such minor decisions.”

“I cannot tolerate your indiscretions,” he said, trembling a bit more. “I absolutely will not make any such decision on your behalf. So please leave.” He pushed a button on his desk twice or thrice.

“Sir, I remind you of the law of karma,” I said. “For every action there is a resulting reaction. So you should be very careful in your dealings regarding Shrii Sarkar.”

Now he was shaking with abandon. “Get out! Get out!” Though he pushed repeatedly on the button, no one disturbed our pleasant conversation.

“Are you familiar, sir, with Ananda Marga’s philosophy and dynamic social work? I think not, and I believe they demand your attention.”

By this time he was unable to think rationally. Rather, he was sweating, shivering and madly swatting the button.

The door opened with a bang. Two men rushed in.

“Grab him! Grab him!” the Vice-Governor yelled.

I was standing still, but they jumped on me like I was a wild coyote, each one grasping an arm of my dangerous body.

“Take him out! Take him away!”

In a few minutes, the three of us arrived at the nearby police station. I sat down while my two captors talked with the police

for a minute and then left.

“You’re under arrest,” a policeman said.

“What’s the charge?”

They talked among themselves. Then the same man, clearly the officer in-charge said, “We don’t know.”

“Well, if you can’t tell me, then I’ll just be leaving.” I stood up.

“No, please, sir. Wait. We shortly find out charge.”

One man went out the door.

After ten minutes he came back, saying, “You charged with attacking Vice-Governor.”

“That’s unmitigated poppycock.” I know that Indians who are uneducated bow their heads to such language. “I didn’t even touch him. I’m going.” I moved toward the door.

“Wait, please wait, sir!” Again the man ran out.

On returning, he said, “Vice-Governor changed charge to threatening attack.”

“Tommyrot and claptrap,” I said. “I was merely discussing philosophy with him. I can’t waste any more time here.” Again I started to leave.

“Wait, sir! Just a minute, please!”

They talked among themselves once more.

Then the officer-in-charge gave me a very feeble smile. “Charges dropped, sir. Please no mention anyone.”

Just after returning to our Central Office, I ran into three Margii brothers on their way for a visit with Baba. Without mentioning what had just happened to me, I asked them to please pass my namaskar[^1] to Him.

Later, coming out of the jail, they told me their story.

They took the trouble to express my regards to Baba and, on their doing so, Baba Himself namaskared with His hands, and

[^1]: *Namaskar* is a hand gesture made with palms together, touching first the forehead and then the heart. It is used when greeting a person, and when parting. It means, “I respect the divinity within you with my mind and my heart.”

then said, “Hmm, Dharmapala.... Just see, just see. Though my boys and my girls are not yet perfectly following *Sixteen Points*,[^4] the immoralists are afraid of them, and literally shake in fear.

“But when my boys and my girls really adhere to *Sixteen Points*,” He continued, “the bones of the immoralists will shake. You understand?”

He jabbed at His own thigh, smiling broadly and saying, “The very bone will shake.”

### Samadhi or not?

Having gone to all possible lengths to gain permission from the authorities to meet Baba, I set upon a new plan, a rather violent one. This time I was determined that nothing would stop me.

Taking permission from the guards, I entered the office of the prison warden. I was well known to him due to my numerous attempts in the past to gain permission. He politely invited me to be seated. One other man was also sitting there.

“I want to tell you, sir,” I said, “that this week I met the Vice-Governor, and he also refused my request to meet Baba. So I’ve decided that if your response to my last-ditch request is negative, then I shall physically thrust you people aside and enter His cell by brute force.” I knew that Baba’s cell was only a few meters away, and that no locked door stood in between. Out of respect, they never locked the door of His cell.

“Sir, sir, you must not think such things. You know I am deeply sorry that I cannot allow you to enter. I would lose my job. I, too, am a devotee of Baba, so please believe me about my limitations.”

“How can you say you’re devoted to Baba? If it were so, then you wouldn’t be afraid to take such a minor risk. You know my heart is breaking to see Him.”

***

[^4]: *Sixteen Points* is a summary of the most important practices suggested for all Margiis.

“We here know all too well about Baba’s power and Omniscience. But I am helpless due to my duty.”

“This is just a load of nonsense. I am going to break through now.” I started to get up.

“Wait! You don’t understand our realization of Baba.” He turned to the other man. “Doctor, please tell this gentleman about the experience you had the other day.”

The doctor cleared his throat. “Usually, I attend Baba every morning at 11:00. But three days ago I was busy all morning and had to delay my visit until 3:00 in the afternoon. When I approached the cell, I got a shock. Baba was sitting in meditation, but He was not sitting on the cot. He was floating about three feet above it! I couldn’t believe my eyes. All my thoughts disappeared, and I stood there, simply staring at him. How much time passed I don’t know. But I slowly became aware that His face was changing. He had become Lord Shiva! Trembling with fear, I ran back to this office.”

“I can attest that he was shaking like a leaf when he dashed in here that day,” said the warden. “I thought he was having an epileptic fit.”

“Well, I don’t care for your explanations or your experiences,” I said. “If you won’t give me permission to enter Baba’s room, then I shall proceed there in my own way.”

Just as I started to rise again, a third man entered the room. The warden turned to me, saying, “Please! Wait at least a moment while I speak to this officer.”

While the warden was occupied, I closed my eyes. Without the slightest effort, I dropped into deep meditation. Losing awareness of my surroundings, I saw Baba smiling sweetly. He was holding me in His lap. Stroking my head repeatedly, He said, “My dear Dharmapala. There is a very good reason why I am not allowing you to meet Me ... a very good reason.”

I became lost in His smile ... His voice ... the feeling of His soft hand.

The next thing I knew I heard a voice saying, “He’s in

*samadhi*.”

I thought, “Who is that? And who is he talking about?”

Gradually I remembered where I was. “Ah, it’s the warden speaking ... speaking about me,” I thought.

I parted my lips, thinking I would say, “No, I’m not in *samadhi*.” But instead of these words, only incoherent mumbles issued from my mouth. I opened my eyes, and tears fell out.

“Wait until you come into normal mood,” the warden said softly. He looked at me with a new gentleness.

A few moments passed while the warden was speaking to the third man. I stood up, and all three of them clearly became apprehensive—on their guard as to what I would do.

“Please reconsider ....” the warden started saying to me.

I cut him off by doing namaskar with my hands, and said in a breaking, feathery voice, “It’s ... alright ... now.”

Almost simultaneously, all three of them dropped their jaws in surprise. They were speechless as I walked out of the office into the outer courtyard.

There, in the sunlight, stood Baba’s personal assistant together with another Dada. They knew of my intention to somehow get into Baba’s cell this day. On seeing my shining, tearful face, they exclaimed, “You’ve seen Baba!”

I did not know whether to say “yes” or “no”.

### Transcending drugs

I waited for news of Baba. In Huskvarna, Sweden, our first three residents moved into our new rehabilitation project. They were all drug addicts. The city government had given us a free lease on the building with an understanding that we would establish a halfway-house for drug addicts.

We only accepted young men who demonstrated an interest to improve themselves. In that case, it was not overly difficult to cure them. Our staff joined them in practicing meditation to gain inspiration and will-power, vegetarianism and fasting to

eliminate body toxins, yoga postures to balance the hormones, and social work to provide a sense of personal value. The system appeared sound, but the most important ingredient was loving care. Enforcing external discipline had little worth in itself.

A few years ago one of our workers first demonstrated this process by curing some heroin addicts in Berlin.

### Playing with danger—an unsolved riddle

I visited Bergen, Norway, for the first time. I stayed in the flat of a brother named Trond, a friend of a friend. He turned out to be a bit ‘different’. It was late when I arrived and he was keen to go to bed, so he showed me a couch where I could sleep. Since I never slept on a soft bed, I instead arranged my blankets on the floor in the corner of his bedroom.

I was sleeping deeply with a cover pulled over my head when something woke me up.

*What is this?* I thought. I felt a pressure on my leg, but could not identify its source. Instantly I regained full alertness. Now I was both curious and anxious.

The pressure was relieved, only to be felt again a moment later on my waist.

*The oddest thing!* I thought. *Is it that fellow up to no good?* Then, with the pressure still against my waist, a second unstable pressure came directly on top of my body.

There’s no mistaking it—surely it’s a person. At first he was walking around me, and now he’s walking on top of me! Whoever it was could not have been expecting anybody to be lying on the floor.

*Though it may be my host simply sleep-walking,* I thought, *it’s more likely a thief moving in the dark.* He took another step, this time on my shoulder. I lay there, unmoving, and thought out a quick plan.

*Now!* I thought, my heartbeat quickening. Quick as a flash, I sat up, simultaneously thrusting the blanket off.

“Uhh!” he exclaimed, clearly shocked that he had been walking on a person. He bucked off me. I saw his eyes widen in fright as he ran toward the door. Without losing a second, I grabbed my pocket knife from my nearby shoulder bag and dashed after him into the adjacent room.

Our scuffle created quite a noise. My host sat up in his bed. “Hey! What’s this? What’re you doing?”

Instead of replying, I shouted to the thief in the most threatening tone I could muster, “Come out! I know you’re here!” I could not see anyone in the darkness.

“What?” Trond yelled. “Who is it?”

I turned on the light and looked all around. Nobody. But there were only the two rooms. And there was no way he could have gone out the door of the flat.

“It’s a thief, or something!” I said to Trond, who was still sitting in bed.

Holding the knife tightly in my fist, I threw aside the curtains, the chairs, tables, everything the thief might use for cover. Nothing. Nobody.

I was dumbfounded. By now Trond had come into the room. I told him in detail what had happened.

“Do you have any explanation?” I said.

“No. It’s pretty strange. Maybe you imagined it all.”

“Impossible. After feeling his first step, I was wide awake.”

“Odd....”

“What? Did you ever experience something like this before?”

“No, no. Surely not.”

With nothing further to speak about, we awkwardly went back to our respective sleeping places. A few minutes passed.

“Ah, Dada,” he said softly. “In fact, something out of the ordinary did happen to me a few nights ago.”

“Tell me.”

“I was fast asleep when I was awakened by someone pressing my body in different places. I threw my blanket off,

and saw someone jump away and run into the other room.”

“But that’s exactly what happened to me!”

“Well ... I ... I don’t know ....”

“What do you think it was?”

“No, no, no ....”

“What?”

“No. Nothing to speak about. Let’s go to sleep.”

He refused to talk any more.

The next morning, while I was doing meditation, Trond left the house.

After meditation, I walked into the second room and looked around. Middle-class conservative furniture ... a small orderly collection of books ... a few slightly gaudy decorative items ... and—wait a moment—I looked at the titles of his books: *History of the Occult*, *Science of Magic*, *Psychic Power Unleashed*, *Hitler and the Spear of Destiny*, *Dictionary of Necromancy*, and many more titles referring to the black arts. What kind of a person was Trond?

Surely the fellow was dabbling in occult power. That might explain the previous night’s phenomenon. The being that walked on me last night was not human, not even physical—that was why he could disappear when I chased him.

It seemed that my host might have developed some psychic power, though it was clear that he was not in control of that power. He, too, was affected by it. On one hand he did not want to talk about it, but on the other hand he wanted help.

Because he did not confide in me, however, and because I had to leave that day, it would have to remain an unsolved riddle—at least for the moment. I could help him, but only if he asked.

Unfortunately, there must be countless other people like him, playing with dangerous powers, hardly knowing what they are doing, learning a few spells and concentration techniques and materializing latent forces from their subconscious minds—before they have the morality, purity and

mental strength to control themselves. What can one do? Nothing, except to continue to develop spiritual and social qualities. At the proper time, society will need and demand the expression of such God-centered qualities.

### Within or without?

Stockholm. After walking alone in the afternoon, up a small hill rising above the buildings and highways of the city, I sat in a natural boulder garden. Surrounded by urban chaos, this huge empty lot with nothing on it except rocks offered itself as a Tantric oasis. Inspired, I resolved not to budge a muscle during meditation.

About half an hour later, it began to drizzle.

*A test, I thought. I shall not move.*

The rain grew stronger, until it became a heavy downpour.

*I can change my clothes any time, I thought, but not my mind.*

It lasted five or ten minutes, and then stopped completely. Again, I became aware of the distant sound of cars creating their usual eerie cosmic wind tunnel effect in my brain. Otherwise the only other sound was the mournful crowing of nearby birds.

My concentration increased until I was no longer conscious of my wetness.

After some time a new, high pitched sound appeared far away, perhaps a kilometer or so. Was it a dog barking faintly? Then a little louder—yes, a dog. Somehow that dog seemed to have something to do with me from the moment I heard it. The barking became louder still. I supposed it was coming toward me.

*Even if it attacks me, I won’t stir.*

Closer and closer the unfriendly bark came. Why? I could not guess. Then it was on my hill, yelping.

*He is going to attack. I don’t care. It’s a test. If I’m hurt or killed, it’s Your problem, Baba.*

The dog was now almost on me. His barking was so loud and vicious that it hurt my eardrums. He was so near to my face that I could feel the heat of his breath, and its stink as well. I sat bolt upright, unmoving. My mind flickered back and forth between the thought of my meditation and that of the dog.

A few seconds later, the noise ceased. A pause. Then I heard his feet, as he turned and walked away from me.

Not allowing myself to wonder how or why it happened, my concentration dived inward. I enjoyed the rest of my meditation.

When I opened my eyes, I looked at my watch. One hour and twenty minutes. I started to rise to my feet. And what? How could it be? There was not a drop of rainwater to be seen. Everything was bone dry, including my clothing.

Could it be that the rain was a figment of my imagination? And the dog also?

I laughed and walked down the hill.

### Devotion and surrender to the Guru

Orebro, Sweden. Every time I received a circular, a letter, or, like on this particular day, a phone call with news from India, I experienced the same feelings. First, my heart fluttered with hope for a positive verdict; then a sinking feeling came when I found out that there had been no real progress. Then the agony of longing to be near Baba would increase until it became a sharp pain in my heart, my face would get hot and a few sighs would escape. Finally, I told myself that there was nothing to worry about, that He knew exactly what He was doing, that it was all just a drama with so many ups and downs that it only seemed like it would never finish. Yet it would, in fact, one day surely come to a happy ending. Then I mentally prostrated to Him, left everything to Him, and grimly turned back to His work. After a few minutes, I was back to normal, encouraging others, smiling and working with as much zeal as I could muster.

Unknown to everyone, my normality also included a constant dull pressure at the back of my skull and in the core of my heart where I buried my yearning to see Him.

### Work while working, meditate while meditating

Stockholm, 1978. Dada Krtashivananda had arrived from India, full of news. Subconsciously I prepared myself to go through my usual sequence of hope, disappointment and frustration. This time I never made it past hope. Baba's case was on a steadily rising list of cases to be heard by the court. *Something concrete should happen any time soon.* To this day, I can still feel that nervous flutter of anticipation in my heart.

Krtashivanandaji was also full of stories. Here I mention two of them.

The first story happened many years ago during a meeting with the Margiis:

BABA: Do you all want to hear the Cosmic Sound?

MARGIIS (about twenty): Yes, yes, Baba!

BABA: Do deep meditation now. *(After a few minutes of silence, Baba asked one Margii.)* What did you hear?

MARGII: I heard the sound of the *Aum*, Baba.

BABA: *(pointing to others)* And you ... and you?

OTHER MARGIIS: Yes, Baba ... and I ... and I ....

*(One by one, each Margii said that he or she heard the cosmic sound, "Aum".)*

BABA: *(pointing to Krtashivanandaji)* And what about you, little boy?

KRTASHIVANANDA: I'm sorry, Baba, I didn't hear anything special.

BABA: Yes. Now you alone, do *dhyana* (higher meditation). *(After a minute)* Well, then?

KRTASHIVANANDA: I'm sorry, Baba. I still could not hear anything.

BABA: I told you to do *dhyana*. Instead, you are thinking

of your missionary work. When doing meditation, you should not think of work. Now do meditation again. *(After another minute)* Hmmm?

KRTASHIVANANDA: Yes, Baba. At last I heard the *Aum*.

BABA: Just see. Just see.

The other story began with a meeting in which Baba assured all the workers that He would never allow any of them to starve. He promised that they would receive at least one meal daily. Therefore there was no need to worry.

Krtashivanandaji wanted to secretly test Baba. During a walking journey which took seven days, he maintained silence. He neither carried any food, nor asked anyone for food. Once each day, however, a different stranger approached him and asked if he needed food. He accepted it without saying anything. This happened every day during those seven days of walking, except on one day—fasting day.

Finally, in June, some real news arrived. Baba's case had started. The prosecution was at last presenting its evidence. Of course, the defense's arguments would follow.

I could hardly stop thinking about Baba. My mind rolled uncontrollably between states of expectation, anxiety, awe (of His cosmic strategy), a desperate craving to see Him, and, occasionally, little flashes of fear.

### It's Baba's problem

I called my higher authority one day and told him, “Dada, ten of the eleven full-timers in my region want to go for acharya training.”

“Very good.”

“So I am planning to send all of them to acharya training this week.”

“All of them?” He sounded either surprised or alarmed.

“Yes. Why not?”

“Are you crazy? If you empty your region of full-timers, all of the work will collapse. Just send two for now. Then perhaps each month you can send another.”

“Look, if Baba wants to help, there’s a local full-timer training session coming up in July. Besides, I thought our most important work is whole-timer creation.”

“That’s right but ....”

“And if I delay in sending some of them, they may lose their inspiration.”

“Don’t be a fool.”

“If any problem comes to the region, it’s Baba’s problem. He has to take care. I am sending all these brothers and sisters for Him.”

“You idiot! I won’t permit you to send them all at once.”

“Dada, excuse me for asking, but is that your suggestion or is that your order?” I asked.

“Well, of course it is not my order. But you’re absolutely not to do it. Do you understand?”

“Yes.”

I knew that he could not give such an order because creating new whole-timer acharyas was our first priority. In this case, I was technically free to make my own decision. Certainly he was right from the standpoint of normal logic. Perhaps I was a fool, but it would be a fine Tantric test for Baba to take care of His own work. I would send them all to the Sweden acharya training center as quickly as possible.

I was in Timmern, Germany. The only full-timer left in my region was Dhruvadev, who did not want to become an acharya. As expected, my higher authority was furious. Baba would have to help me.

The new Berlin Sector full-timer training session had started. I was the trainer together with another Dada. It was a one-month program, and the biggest we had ever held. Ninety Margiis were attending. Thirty of them were from Scandinavia.

Of course, most of the trainees were only here for the experience. We would have to see how many Baba would inspire to become real full-timers.

### Free at last!

News had arrived!!!! What news!!!! It was the happiest day of my life!!!! Baba had at last been released!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!!

In July, news had come from Patna that the defense had started presenting their arguments. Only Baba knew what would happen, what He had planned.

But finally, after seven years of imprisonment and over five years of fasting, He was vindicated, absolved of guilt!!!!

The true criminal was Indira Gandhi’s administration. By using deceit, bribery, corruption, intimidation, torture, defamation—what to speak of a total ban against Ananda Marga and imprisonment of all Margiis and workers—Mrs Gandhi and her cohorts had tried their best to finish off Baba and Ananda Marga but they had failed miserably. History would document all the steps that the forces of Nature would take against the real sinners. We needed to do nothing against them, nor could we hate them. Though they were full of negativity, they too were unwitting tools of God’s play.

My wonderful Baba! I still had not physically seen Him. They said that He was withered and emaciated—maintained only by His psycho-spiritual power. Now His fast would finish, and He would surely regain His robust constitution. Clouds cannot long overcast the sun.

And I would see Him in person at long last!!!! It had been a long wait for seven years.

Yes, I would go to India. But first I had to finish the local full-timer training session which was going perfectly.

### Guidance from afar

Timmern, on 8 August. I had been tortured by asthma for many nights. Deep within, I did not mind. It was an interesting

test. But it was my duty to try to cure it, so I had experimented with many remedies. The previous day, I even began a cure recommended by our local Margii doctor, Sukumar, in which I was supposed to twice daily cleanse my intestines by drinking my own urine. I did it but found it so repulsive that I discontinued it this very day. Baba Himself would have to instruct me to undergo this treatment before I would take it up again.

Ten days later, a circular arrived from India, highlighting many new points given by Baba. For me, a most interesting one was that He criticized Indian Prime Minister Morarji Desai's daily health habit of drinking his own urine. Desai often openly declared its curative value. Baba, however, said that urine was the most crude substance one could ingest.

The circular was dated 9 August. This meant that He had directly caught my thought: "Baba, You have to personally instruct me if You want me to resume this cure." But instead of telling me to resume it, He forbade it.

Of course, there was no way that news of my experiment could have been conveyed to Calcutta.

My experience, about two years later, paralleled the above one. It also happened in Germany.

The Dadas and Didis had come from all over the sector for RDS. To break the tension one evening, a few of us went to a movie. Once there, it was certainly our duty to maximally enjoy ourselves, and eliminate the greatest possible tension. Accordingly, we laughed our heads off.

Little did we know that the local district secretary of Ananda Marga was also present in the same theater. The next day, he complained to our higher authority that "The Dadas' behavior was unsuitable for acharyas."

Almost immediately, a circular came from India with a few new conduct rules from Baba, including: "Acharyas must not go to public movie theaters."

Without going into detail, I am one hundred percent sure that no one reported our pleasurable evening at the movies to India. Considering that the circular was dated the day afterwards, I believe that this was yet another instance of Baba's sticking His adorably ethereal nose into our personal lives. Having an all-knowing guru has both its advantages and disadvantages!

### Perfect work

Full-timer training ended. Baba had inspired ten of the thirty trainees from Scandinavia to become full-timers—exactly filling the gap created by the previous ones who had left to become acharyas. Another example of perfect work by the Mystic Sculptor. Ten for ten! His blessing was clear: He reveled in this kind of noble risk.

Before leaving for India, I would place all the new full-timers in the field.

Yes, my flight was booked for India, and this time I would see Baba. Nothing could stop our meeting now.... Unless He played some nasty last-minute trick! *Not this time, please.*

### Having fun with a bad man

Copenhagen. After we finished kiirtan and began group meditation in the evening, I felt something evil in the air. Though I had never stood up during group meditation before, this time I made an exception. Without disturbing everyone's meditation, I quietly walked into the front room which served as our cooperative restaurant and community center. Mainjula was sitting there.

"Did anything strange happen just now, Mainjula?" I whispered.

"No, Dada...."

"Have you been completely alone?"

"Well, a man came in. He looked around for a couple of minutes, and left just before you came in."

“Who was he?”

“I don’t know his name. He was Indian and has been here a few times before. He has a mustache, and....”

“I know,” I interrupted. “I’ve seen him several times recently. He’s thin, has a sharp chin, and beady eyes which he shifts around as he speaks. He expresses an exaggerated interest in meditation and yoga though he’s never tried to learn.”

“Yeah, that’s him.”

“What was he doing just now?”

“Nothing.”

“He must be a very bad man. His very vibration strongly disturbed my meditation. If you ever see him again, please tell me.”

Two days later, I got ready to fly off. However, I was so late for my Aeroflot flight to Moscow in the morning that the plane had to be delayed a few minutes only for me. Who could believe that I would be late for a flight that was taking me directly to Baba? But there was so much to take care of before I left. I had to be either responsible and late, or irresponsible and on-time. Did He always have to make such last minute dramas?

Once they rushed me on board, the stewardess escorted me directly to my seat.

With all the hurry, I did not notice the passenger sitting next to me until I had already put on my seat belt—it was the same Indian man who had disturbed my meditation two days before. I was astonished, but immediately understood the connection. Surely he was a member of the CBI (Central Bureau of Investigation in India) with instructions to follow me. How could he be foolish enough to reserve a seat next to mine?

*Damn, I thought. Is this yet another of Baba’s tricks to keep me from seeing Him?*

“Nice to see you again,” he said with a derisive smirk on his face.

“Where are you going?” I asked politely.

“To Delhi, of course.”

“What takes you to Delhi?”

“To meet my family. And where are you going?” He was still wearing the same arrogant grin. Confident that I was also going to Delhi, he no longer had anything to hide—in contrast to the last few days during which he had shadowed me in Copenhagen.

“To Dacca,” I said bluntly, staring at him. The look on his face abruptly turned to bewilderment when he realized that he had miscalculated.

“And though I appreciate your recent concern for my security,” I continued, “I can’t figure out what you hope to find out from me. Nevertheless, you’ll have to excuse me because my curiosity is less than my repulsion for this kind of game.”

I stood up and moved to another seat. Something told me that this man might soon lose his job. But it was not my duty to look after *his* security.

### Personal Contact at last!

Calcutta. How my heart pounded as I waited for Baba upstairs in the Jodhpur Park office. Would He be the same as I had seen in my dreams? Would He smile as I had imagined? How would He treat me? What would He say? They said that He would come soon—now what had delayed Him? After waiting seven years to see Him, seven minutes was agony!

Thirty workers lined up in the corridor. Some gossiped or hummed a tune. But not a sound could pass my lips; nothing could enter my mind except the thought of Him.

*“Parampita Baba ki jai! Victory to Baba!”*

Suddenly—there He was! Alive. Breathing. Walking towards me. Not a vision or a dream this time. He took over my eyes, my mind. Every muscle, every nerve leaned towards Him as He moved down the silent row. Internally, I was simultaneously crying, laughing, shouting. The desires, the anxieties, the aching thirst of so many years in those few

seconds were relieved. No thought, no word—except “Baba!!” That for which I was born—fulfilled. If in that moment I had died, and fallen at His feet, I would have been satisfied.

Who can understand such feelings? Who has felt the invisible pull, the magnetism of the guru? It is transcendental. Beyond explanation. Guru’s attraction is said by all the spiritual poets to be inexpressibly greater than even the greatest human love.

He gave me a passing glance. He saw me. I was stunned. I did not need that but He gave it. Everything which followed in my life would be like toys for an infant already suckling at its mother’s breast.

He walked into His room. The door closed. I remained like a puppet with a head full of sawdust.

Then excitement, voices echoing meaninglessly down the corridor, and one sound pierced through the din: “Those who have not yet had Personal Contact, come here!” I drifted toward Dada Ramananda, Baba’s personal assistant. Only Indian workers and full-timers were around him.

“What, you?” he said to me. “You haven’t had Personal Contact yet?”

“No, Dadaji.”

“All these years?” Without another word he turned sharply, opened Baba’s door, and went in.

Within seconds he reappeared, grabbed my shoulders, and shoved me through the door. “Go in!” … I stumbled, and caught myself while the door shut behind me.

I looked up. He sat alone on His bed, smiling. I threw myself at His feet, extending my arms on the floor before Him until I was like an arrow piercing the target.

“Sit up, my boy.” He was speaking to me! Was I dreaming? Tears began to flow from my eyes. Oh, what would He say now? I had waited seven years for this! A strong vibration surrounded Baba, surrounded me, and permeated the room—something I had never felt before.

“What is your name, my boy?” What—was this a joke? He knew not only my name, but everything, everything about me, more than I knew about myself.

I smiled and said, “Dharmapala, Baba.” How silly.

“And what is your posting?”

*Oh, come to the point, Baba*, I thought. *Talk to me personally, not like someone You never met!*

Again I smiled. “Regional Secretary of Stockholm and Oslo Regions, Baba.”

“Acha. You know you made some mistakes in your past.”

I smiled, saying, “Yes, Baba.” Now, surely He would go into detail about my personal history. The energy coming from Him promised something special was about to happen.

But no—what I had hoped for, what I expected was not to be. A few minutes passed, some more words about correcting myself, about becoming a model for others. A threatened punishment with His stick, the stick whistling through the air, and stopping just before touching me. An oath. Formalities—all formalities.

The Personal Contact had finished. Again I lay at His feet, and then left.

I had waited seven years for Him to ask me my name and my posting? My heart sank. *I am nothing special to Baba*, I thought. The blood rushed to my head. Did I only imagine His greatness all these years?

Dumbfounded, I stood again outside His door, but this time there was doubt. Doubt—ugly and dark.

However, I had little time to brood. Ramanandaji went inside Baba’s room, then came out quickly and said, “Personal Contact is finished today. Get ready for *darshan*.”

*Darshan*—to see: a time when all were invited to see Him, or be seen by Him. We all rushed up to the roof.

Already several hundred people were sitting there. Following no one’s example, I moved to the front, and sat immediately in front of Baba’s sofa.

Why had He talked like that to me? I felt cheated. Okay, my work had been for humanity. But it was also to please Him. And He did not care. I was just another piece from His toolbox.

He came and sat down. We danced kiirtan in front of Him.

Still I would try to please Baba.

We danced, we sang, we sat down, and He began speaking.

And then—what was this? He looked deep within me, His eyes twinkled, His lips turned into a smile, He put His hand to my face, He gently pinched my cheek, saying, “Yes, yes. And what do you say, my little boy?” I was speechless, smiling back. He lightly slapped my face, lovingly.

Ecstasy! I was special to Him after all! He loved me!

If my smile had been any bigger, my face would have broken.

He went on talking. Glancing at me again and again. And again He pinched me and lightly slapped me.

Though hundreds of others were there, we might as well have been alone. This time He was personal to the extreme.

He left. Again I was baffled, but this time it was sweet chaos. Why did He play such games? Clearly, He loved me. But in the Personal Contact itself, He said nothing interesting, and did nothing memorable. Afterward, only afterward, He was indeed very loving—beyond my imagination. Why?

Slowly, I began to understand. Personal Contact was spiritual. Purely spiritual and vibrational. It did not matter what happened experientially. Experience was not spiritual but mental. He did what He wanted during the Personal Contact. It would have exactly the proper unique effect on me, unrelated to either understanding or misunderstanding.

And I knew—that He wanted me to tell others that Personal Contact was purely spiritual and that one should not expect anything. He would do only what was necessary to deepen our consciousness, which was beyond any objective phenomena.

My head spun. My *samskaras*[^5] rose up, danced, and accelerated to the speed of life.

Baba—the Tantra guru—cared only for that spirituality. And He pinched me, He slapped me. Why? Just to please me.

He did what He wanted—and then He did what I wanted. I realized that He was wise beyond imagination and my heart overfilled with love for Him.

### Persecution—the norm for great personalities

It seems that all of history’s great Tantrics had to undergo either great suffering or great austerities. Buddha lived as an ascetic and later fasted for forty days. Krishna was born in jail and persecuted throughout much of his life. Jesus embraced poverty, was tormented throughout the years of his missionary work, and underwent the harshest torture on the cross. Seven thousand years ago, the first known Tantric guru, Shiva, was so badly treated and insulted that his wife, out of protest, burned herself to death.

Baba was no different. After seven years in prison, a poisoning that would have killed anyone else, and more than

[^5]: For every action there is a reaction. Until the reaction occurs, the unexpressed reaction awaits expression. This unexpressed reaction is termed *samskara*. Sooner or later it must be expressed. Every thought is also an action, and is like a seed sown in the mind, changing the mind from its original equilibrium. A reaction is needed to return to that equilibrium. When the mind’s balance is disturbed, an opposite expression of an equivalent quantity of energy is thus required. If there is a delay in time, “equivalent quantity” takes that into account, and often requires a greater suffering or pleasure in order to balance the original disturbance. It is something like interest accrued in a bank account over time. Due to psychic suppression or repression, a person may have difficulty expressing *samskaras*. One may have mental blocks or fear. This causes a slowing down of spiritual development. Such blocks are to some extent inevitable in every person because of our human weaknesses. Because the very presence of Baba caused a strong stir in everyone’s mind, Margiis and workers always experienced an increase in the speed of expression of their *samskaras* just after seeing Baba. This was especially true when one had Personal Contact.

five years of fasting, He picked up the work of building His mission and running the organization as if He had merely gone out for a walk.

### The Guru teaches in unexpected ways

Before I explain about the reporting session with Baba, I want to write a little introductory material. Two or three years before I joined Ananda Marga, I began reading spiritual books. One of the first was the biography of Milarepa, the best known Tantric in the history of Tibet. Milarepa’s guru, Marpa, severely tested him even before giving him initiation. The guru alternated between ignoring him, treating him brutally, and making fun of him. Milarepa was ordered to build a house of stones. This back-breaking work took him many months. When he completed it, the guru ridiculed him, and told him to build it again in a different way and in a different spot. This happened six times. Besides treating Milarepa severely, the guru even pretended to be drunk.

Finally, Milarepa’s despair overcame him. He left his guru and went to another teacher. A few days later, he realized his mistake, returned to his guru, begged forgiveness, and pleaded for the initiation. The guru replied, “If only you had built one more house, your ego would have shrunk to the proper size. You would have burned most of your samskaras. After initiation, you would have achieved liberation within a short time. Now I am forced to give you initiation, but you will have to practice meditation and austerities for many years to get your self-realization.”

In the following years, Milarepa lived in below-freezing conditions without clothing, ate no food except evergreen nettle soup, and practiced long meditation in lonely mountain caves. During this time, his guru died. Milarepa persisted until he achieved his goal. He then gradually created a large school of disciples. In his later life, though he underwent painful diseases which were said to be beyond the endurance of normal human

beings, he was always in a blissful mood.

From that young age, I understood that the spiritual path gradually demands greater and greater commitment. The goal is reached only if one is prepared to sacrifice everything for God.

Tantric scriptures specify that a true guru’s relationship with a disciple must swing according to need from strictness and strong punishment to intimacy and affection.

There are similarities to this concept in many traditions which possess elements of Tantra. Both Chinese and Japanese Zen owe their origins directly to Tantra. One of the most renowned Zen masters, Linji Yixuan (in Japanese: Rinzai), who lived in the ninth century, was almost legendary for using anger to awaken his disciples. He said, “Sometimes a shout is like the precious sword of the Diamond King; sometimes a shout is like a golden-haired lion that creeps forward in a crouch; sometimes a shout is like a lure stick with a tuft of grass dangling on the end; sometimes a shout is not used as a shout at all.”

Another guru, Ekido, was a particularly severe teacher. His pupils feared him. One of them, who was on duty striking the gong to tell the time of day, missed his beats when his eye was attracted by a beautiful girl passing by the temple gate. At that moment, Ekido, who was directly behind him, hit him with a stick and the shock happened to kill him. Ekido’s attitude remained absolutely unchanged by this incident. After this took place, he was able to produce under his guidance more than ten enlightened successors, a very unusual number.

Of course, I do not condone such a killing, and rather consider that it might have been due to Ekido’s carelessness. I simply mention it to demonstrate that harshness from the side of the teacher is a normal technique, and does not necessarily indicate a loss of control. As far as I know, Baba’s punishments never produced any injury, not even a slight injury. In fact, I once heard Him mention that, whatever methods He used, they

always resulted in an improvement in health.

Yet another example concerns the master Inzan, who showed no differentiation in his treatment of his disciple Gisho, on account of her sex. He scolded her like a thunderstorm. He cuffed her to awaken her inner nature. After her enlightenment, Inzan wrote a poem in her honor:

> This nun studied thirteen years under my guidance.
> In the evening she considered the deepest koans,
> In the morning she was wrapped in other koans.
> The Chinese nun Tetsuma surpassed all before her,
> And since Mujaku none has been so genuine as this Gisho!
> Yet there are many more gates for her to pass through.
> She should receive still more blows from my iron fist.

The very founder of Tantra, Shiva, had the habit of thrashing his leading disciples with a burning stick. And it was said that his highest blessings were given through that burning stick, and that those who received his beating became the foremost social and spiritual leaders of the time.

Now I turn back to Baba. There are many stories about His reporting sessions, the countless displays of His spiritual power and love, and the punishment He meted out to His workers. A reporting session with Baba was something extraordinary, always. For those who never experienced it, no words can adequately describe it. From the organizational standpoint, it served as an occasion for Baba to examine our work output, and give guidance for improvements. More significantly, it was a time for us to be close to our guru, and for Him to stir into our hearts whatever spiritual ingredients we needed. Part of His method for doing this involved stimulating different emotions in us, like shame, fear, love, anger, anxiety and compassion. His techniques for doing this changed constantly and continuously. All of our work targets were difficult and often

impossible, thus giving Baba plenty of opportunity to isolate the real causes for our failures. And those causes were always psychic weaknesses. In one way or another, by subtle indirect methods, He brought out these weaknesses, and then helped us to overcome them.

Over the years I experienced that each time I headed back to my own field from meeting Baba, I always felt cleansed, renewed, stronger, and more pure. Indeed, all the Margiis used to notice and comment how changed and bright anyone’s face was after coming back from being with Baba abroad—no matter how much harshness or tenderness they had apparently experienced.

I want to add something more still about Baba’s incomparable ability to alternatively love and scold us. For this purpose I take the liberty to quote from an article by Dada Sarveshvarananda, a previous General Secretary. He wrote:

> Baba was as strong as thunder in dealing with evil or immoral actions on the one hand, and as tender as a flower bud in dealing with righteous or moral actions on the other. Actually, I never felt Baba’s anger was in any sense like that of an ordinary person. Usually if someone loses his temper, his blood pressure rises, his face turns red and he loses all mental equilibrium. But Baba was always in control of Himself. He would show anger and displeasure to make us realize our faults and goad us on towards inner and outer perfection. I always felt that He was playing a role with His anger for us because, in the next moment, He could be light-hearted again—laughing and making us laugh.
>
> Truly, we were not as unnerved by His anger as one might expect. Even though that anger blew through us like a devastating storm, we knew that soothing rainfall was sure to follow. The severity of the prolonged reproofs and condemnation we had to face, during our reporting sessions,

pierced through our minds like arrows and made us completely heartbroken. We would then feel hopelessly rejected. But when the reporting was over, He would change completely. He would call us and shower loving caresses and sweet, calming words on us. This love, this affection, was so sublime and touching that all the humiliation, dejection and agonies we were experiencing a moment before were instantly gone.

Baba once said to me, “No matter how high a position a person attains, he or she will always need a strict guardian to answer to for his or her deeds—good or bad—who will give him or her proper guidance in life’s journey. That guardian will also be a perennial source of inspiration. That is why I have a responsibility to be very strict in my discipline and duties. But it is not my real nature. My responsibility compels me, against my wish, to be harsh with you.” Infinite affection was His real nature. What we saw in the way of anger and fury was nothing but a camouflage for an inner ocean of love and affection for all.

Further, here is a story by Dada Tapeshvarananda:

In 1984, I was a Central worker. During the few days that the General Secretary was absent, I had the duty to give most of the reports to Baba. In the collective sessions, Baba gave me terrible punishment, as if the whole blame for the organization’s defects was mine.

After one punishment, I felt so wounded, both physically and mentally, that I wanted to distance myself from Him. I decided I would not sing Prabhat Samgiit, and that I would do only organizational work, since that was all He seemed to care about.

So that night when Baba returned from field walk, I intentionally avoided Him, and was not there to sing with everyone else. I heard that Baba asked, “Where is Tapeshvarananda?”

Someone answered, “Baba, he was just here, but maybe he is busy somewhere.”

After eating His dinner, He called me. I could not avoid it and had to go to His room. I did not look at Baba, and kept my eyes down while I answered His questions.

He asked, “How did you like that song I gave yesterday, *Tumi amar dhyaner dhyeyo?* Did you learn it?” I was silent.

“You cannot remember?”

“No, Baba, I did not learn it.”

“Whatever you remember, even one or two lines, you sing.”

Then I could not control my tears, and started crying. I said, “Baba, I did not learn the song, I cannot remember any line.”

“Why?” I could not reply.

He said, “You see, I understand. You may feel that I only punish you, I only torture you. But you don’t understand that when I am punishing you, my inner intention is not to torture you, but to purify you. You may feel externally that it is torture, that it is humiliation. But spiritual purification comes after suffering, torture and humiliation.

“The main enemy on the spiritual path is ego, and ego can be overcome only through these three processes of suffering, torture and humiliation. You may feel bad, but you don’t know how happy I am when I see that you are successfully passing all these sufferings and tortures, because bliss and the supreme Ananda come only after that. Ultimately, in the spiritual world, nothing is suffering. There is only you moving toward the spiritual bliss.

“Do you know why I asked you about that song? The last line says, ‘I am weeping. Is that what You want? If that gives You pleasure, then I will go on weeping only for You.’”

So after this account about Baba’s strictness, let us turn to what really happened at the reporting session.

I had heard stories about reporting sessions in front of Baba, the punishment He meted out to his workers and the countless

displays of His spiritual power and love; I had my first real taste during a session with the education department.

The melodrama went thus:

GENERAL SECRETARY [GS]: How many schools were started last month in your region?

RANCHI REGIONAL SECRETARY [REG. SEC]: Three, Dada.

BABA: Why only three? How many of your diocese secretaries are present here?

REG. SEC: Four, Baba.

BABA: *(frowning and squinting)* Then why not four schools? Nonsense, rascal. Who is the worker that didn’t start a school?

DIOCESE SECRETARY [DIO. SEC]: *(stepping forward uneasily)* Myself, Baba.

BABA: Is there any justification for such gross inefficiency?

DIO. SEC: *(stammering)* I try ... tried my ... my best, Baba.

BABA: Tried! Stupid. One does or does not do. To sincerely try is to do. So no need to keep the word *try* in your dictionary. Ranchi regional secretary, come forward! *(The REG. SEC steps in front of Baba.)* Due to your inadequate supervision, this jewel-of-a-boy’s potentiality was not fully utilized. Hands up! *(The REG. SEC lifts both his arms straight up.)* Animal! Only eating and sleeping! *(Baba hits His stick against his side. The REG. SEC jumps up involuntarily.)* Wasting your time and misguiding your workers! *(Baba strikes him a couple more times, as the REG. SEC mutters, “Baba, Baba” and leaps from side to side.)*

DIO. SEC: *(moving closer to Baba)* No, don’t beat him, Baba! It’s my fault.

BABA: *(pausing with the stick and speaking in a calm, dignified manner)* No, it’s not your fault. It is due to your supervisor. *(He turns to the REG. SEC and strikes him once*

*more.)* Idiot, lazy fellow!

REG. SEC: *(speaking to the DIO. SEC)* Say something concrete!

DIO. SEC: Baba, I'll start a school within one month.

BABA: *(hits the REG. SEC again)* One month! One month! Do you think that the suffering humanity can wait for such listlessness and lethargy?

DIO. SEC: One week, Baba! I'll start the school within one week!

BABA: *(halting with the stick)* Did you hear what the boy said, GS?

GS: Yes, Baba. He said he will start a school in one week.

BABA: *(taking out a handkerchief to wipe the sweat from His brow)* Yes, make a note. If a school is not started within one week, then further measures will be required.

REG. SEC: Baba, I will properly supervise him.

BABA: Yesss....

Surely most people would be horrified to see such a display of anger and force. But I was full of inspiration. Here was a man-making guru, capable of molding His disciples for the benefit of society.

After leaving the room, the Regional Secretary joked and laughed, his face suffused with joy, as if nothing serious had happened, and somehow the marks of the stick had disappeared. It made me even more curious to know the inner effect of His stick.

### The Guru uplifts

Later I got a chance to ask one senior worker how everyone tolerated Baba's abuse. He said, "We know from Tantra's long tradition that the guru has the responsibility to uplift his disciple from animal-life to warrior-life to divine-life. To achieve this, the guru's behavior will have to fluctuate between extremely bitter and extremely sweet. And it varies for each

disciple.[^6]

"More importantly, Baba instructed us on how to deal with subordinate workers. He said that for every ten parts of strictness we use, we must give at least eleven parts of love. In His case, I feel like all the strictness He employs cannot compare with His boundless love. Baba's existence is only for us. He does nothing for Himself. It doesn't matter if someone else believes I'm right or wrong about Baba, because that's my daily experience. That's why no amount of harsh treatment can shake my relationship with Him."

After the Dada left, I remembered a story I had heard about Swami Shivananda. One of his disciples once asked him a question, "Guruji, your teaching is beautiful for all of us who are practicing yoga and meditation. Your mission also benefits thousands of sick people who come to our medical clinics. But what about the rest of the human society, the millions and billions who suffer from poverty, ignorance and injustice? Can you not do something for them? Can you not please guide us to help the entire society?"

Shivanandaji answered, "We must only help the rest of suffering humanity indirectly. To serve them directly would require a vast organization which would crumble under its own weight. My workers would quarrel with one another and destroy whatever was created. No, I am not the man to do that work."

I believe that Baba's greatest contribution to history is the creation of a vast Tantric organization to serve the entire human society, an organization based on renunciate workers.

[^6]: Everyone has different needs—has their own path. Over the years I saw that some Dadas were frequently harshly scolded by Baba, some Dadas never received a single scolding, while most were in between. As to His treatment of Didis—they were never beaten on their bodies. They only felt His stick on their open palms. Nevertheless, though their physical pain was negligible, it somehow caused them to open up, surrender and change as much as the Dadas.

Because renunciates prefer to live outside of normal social disciplines, the subtlest psychology is required in training us. More importantly, for this organization to succeed, we have to overcome our petty differences. We have to move together as one great family. For this purpose, Baba belittled our egos by chastising us, and encouraged our souls to unfold by loving us.

**Awakening latent qualities with His magic stick**

I did not have to wait long for my own personal experience. The day came when I, too, felt the touch of His stick.

BABA: What is your post?
ME: Regional Secretary, Stockholm and Oslo Regions, Baba.
BABA: How many new kindergartens or primary schools did you open in the last one month?
ME: *(feeling very proud)* Two kindergartens, Baba.
BABA: And in that same period how many permanent welfare centers did you start?
ME: Ah, none, Baba.
BABA: Do you think the number is adequate?
ME: No, Baba.
BABA: Are you proud of your work?
ME: No, Baba.
BABA: Should we all praise you?
ME: No, Baba. You should punish me.
BABA: Yes, you deserve punishment. Hands up! *(I raise my hands. Baba strikes me on my right side. I am surprised by the intensity of the pain and jump slightly.)* Are you properly utilizing your time as a worker?
ME: No, Baba. *(As He goes on hitting, my mind and body involuntarily settle down as I look into His eyes. Instead of reflecting anger, those eyes are compassionate.)* I will do better, Baba, much better.
BABA: What does he say, GS?

GENERAL SECRETARY [GS]: He says he will do much better, Baba.

BABA: *(switching over to hitting me on my left side)* This reply is not sufficient.

ME: *(Though the pain is real, I feel my mind diving deeper into Him.)* I shall work every second of every minute, Baba. *(more blows)* I will not think for my own petty self. I will become an ideal man.

BABA: *(turning the edges of His mouth upward, His cheeks dimpling)* GS, he *does* want to be a good boy. Yes. *(waving His stick toward the side of the room)* Go, stand there on the side.

I went and joined the Dadas who had already received Baba’s treatment. Amazingly, the intense pain was almost completely gone. Rather, I was feeling overwhelmed with the strong desire to serve humanity to my utmost capacity. And my affection for Baba was so strong that it seemed to be physically pressing out against my chest.

### Right, not wrong

Mahindra used to serve as one of Baba’s bodyguards. When he heard that I had just begun to experience the stick, he told me a story from the time that Baba was in jail:

My old friend, Awadhanath Prasad, begged me to arrange a meeting with Baba. He told me he had done something bad with a lady who worked in the fields under his supervision. He had committed other sins too.

When we entered His cell, Baba immediately yelled at Awadhanath, “Why did you come? Animal, pig!”

“I came due to Mahindra.”

“Mahindra, why did you bring this nasty boy?”

“Baba, please help him.”

“Bring me my stick.”

I looked around the cell but I could not find Baba’s stick, so

I borrowed the constable’s and gave it to Baba. Right there in front of the prison guards, Baba beat Awadhanath. Then He told him to rub his nose on the floor, which he did until it bled.

Afterwards, when we went outside, the CID (Central Intelligence Department) wanted Awadhanath to file a case against Baba for beating him, but he refused, saying, “He is my guru! What He did was right, not wrong!”

After that, he became a completely pure and exemplary man. He spent all his spare time doing social service.

### Power comes from difficulties

Margiis and workers were present from all over the world. Just think! When Baba was arrested in December, 1971, there were Margiis in only five countries. At this time, seven years later, Ananda Marga was active in over eighty countries.

The Tantric guru and his disciples always gain power from their difficulties. Every effort made by the Gandhi regime to destroy Ananda Marga eventually resulted in strengthening our mission.

This reminds me of three statements Baba made while still in prison. The first was shortly after His arrest. He was asked when He would come out of jail. He replied, “Only when Ananda Marga is sixteen times stronger.” At that time I thought He meant when we strictly followed “16 Points”. But I later saw that at least one level of meaning was the number of countries in which Ananda Marga was working—5 countries before Baba's imprisonment, multiplied by 16, became 80 countries on His release from prison.

The second statement was during the emergency rule, when He was convicted by a kangaroo court (in which we were not permitted to have any witnesses) and sentenced to life imprisonment. In that seemingly darkest of moments, He turned to His attorney, smiled, and wrote on His message

board: “Now the tables will turn.”[^7] Soon after, Indira Gandhi lost her power, and our workers and Baba were vindicated.

The other statement occurred at the end of the emergency, when the ban against Ananda Marga was lifted and our workers were released from jail. Many of them had undergone great suffering. This was especially true of those whom the authorities had physically and psychically punished in an effort to obtain written denunciations of Ananda Marga. Baba’s comment at that time was, “The workers have passed through the blazing crucible. Their iron has been forged into steel. Previously they (Gandhi and others) believed Ananda Marga to be a dangerous baby snake. Now, thanks to them, it has become a fully grown snake.”

### My new posting

After working as the Scandinavian regional secretary for nearly three years, I was transferred to Stockholm. My posting was to a section which previously did not exist outside of India: Volunteers Service Department or SD. I began my duties as the European Chief Secretary of SD. The programs of SD included physical social services for the needy, survival training, security, relevant higher philosophy, and training in basic service-skills such as first aid. Among the means for providing this training were weekend SD camps, which also encouraged collective discipline and unity through group exercises.
